# W0.2 — Core public UUID contract

Design/audit only, 2026-10-16 (Asia/Kolkata). Branch `feat/native-user-management`; HEAD `2f48ff835d9e692fc84b31aaa951aad2495888ac`, unchanged from Phase 1/W0.1. Scope is **only tenants, projects, products, logical_sbom, sbom_source and sbom_component**. References to those six resources on other routers are included; identities of users, memberships, runs, findings, jobs, reports, lookup tables and other resources are dependencies, not redesigned here.

**Proposed contract:** keep integer PK/FKs, internal tenant context and service/job arguments; add immutable public UUIDv4 mappings to these six tables; emit UUIDv4 strings under existing public ID field names. Resolve public UUIDs within authorized scope before passing integers to existing services. This is a reviewable proposal, not an implemented API contract or deployment approval.

**Readiness: GO for the next scoped design review / W1 disposable-baseline design. NO-GO for code, schema, clone creation, migration execution or public cutover under this task.** W0.1's disposable-target verification/approval and schema-drift gates remain open. Identity/JWT subject redesign is explicitly deferred; no token semantics are changed or prescribed by W0.2.

## Inputs, evidence and limits

Read the complete [Phase 1 final audit](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/docs/uuid-migration/phase1/09-final-audit.md:1), [impact CSV](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/docs/uuid-migration/phase1/09-impact-matrix.csv:1), [API inventory](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/docs/uuid-migration/phase1/05-api-exposure.md:1), [frontend inventory](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/docs/uuid-migration/phase1/06-frontend.md:1) and [W0.1 readiness report](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/docs/uuid-migration/phase2/00-deployment-target-readiness.md:1), with targeted current source inspection. No application modules, database configuration, engine or services were executed. No database connection was made in W0.2.

The field appendix preserves **964 scoped binding rows across 263 route registrations** from audit 05. These are registration/direction/type occurrences, not 964 distinct fields or verified runtime leaks; optional unions and aliases remain explicit. Routes present only because they share a tenant header are included for that header, without expanding their other resource contracts. The route index retains exact registration evidence and alias notes. All references are source evidence; no runtime OpenAPI/request or browser validation was performed. Dynamic JSON, text reports and historical payloads remain evidence gaps.

At start, 20 tracked files were modified and 12 files were untracked (the 11 Phase 1/W0.1 documents plus the existing frontend test). All were preserved. The branch and SHA were checked before work; no upstream refresh or remote-sync assertion is made. Existing edits are part of the inspected working tree. Only this Markdown report is added.

## Entity identity and representation

For each table, propose a native PostgreSQL `uuid` `public_id`, generated with random UUIDv4, unique per table, non-null after a separately designed validated backfill, immutable and never reused. It is a non-PK mapping. Generation must cover every writer; the database default/function and backfill details remain W1 work subject to target verification. No integer-derived UUID, UUIDv5 or random-on-read value. A new logical SBOM, SBOM evidence/version row or component record gets its own public UUID; version/restore/conversion creates preserve existing record-creation semantics and never reuse another row's UUID.

The outward `id` field is the table's `public_id` serialized as a canonical lowercase hyphenated UUIDv4 string. Do not return internal `id` beside it. Public parent/child `*_id` fields carry the target table's public UUID; nullability and array ordering stay as currently defined. No requirement to add a public JSON `public_id` field in parallel with `id`. OpenAPI proposal: `type: string`, `format: uuid`, plus documented/enforced version-4 and RFC variant validation (format alone does not ensure v4). Accept standard hyphenated UUID text case-insensitively and canonicalize output; reject numeric IDs, nil UUIDs, wrong versions, compact hex/braced/URN alternatives under this proposed wire grammar. Final validation policy is subject to contract review, not assumed deployed.

## Resolution and authorization contract

1. Authenticate and enforce the existing method/path permission policy. Resolve the requested tenant UUID through the current principal's active, eligible memberships; bind the existing **integer** `CurrentContext.tenant_id`. Never bind a UUID to `TenantSession`, weaken its exact-int validation, or trust a header/cookie as authorization. Preserve account-disabled/suspended, tenant status, membership and revocation precedence. Current selection compares decimal ID/slug/external mapping; proposed selection uses only the local public UUID in the normal public selector. Compatibility with external/slug hints is an unresolved explicit dependency, not an automatic fallback.
2. Select each owned resource by `resource.public_id = parsed_uuid AND resource.tenant_id = context.tenant_id` and existing operation-specific active/deleted/lifecycle predicates. Return the row or necessary projection, not just an integer that requires an immediate duplicate fetch. Pass the resolved integer ID/row into existing service contracts. Resource type comes from the field/route contract, never a search across tables for the same UUID.
3. Validate nested ownership in the same statement where practical: product.project_id matches authorized project, logical.product_id matches selected product, source.logical_sbom_id and product/project relationships agree, component.sbom_id matches selected SBOM; all share the selected tenant. Same-tenant wrong-parent inputs must fail as unavailable within that scope. Do not silently reparent records during UUID conversion. Preserve explicit supported reassignment and current-SBOM eligibility rules through the existing services/locking.
4. Tenant itself is the authorization root: ordinary tenant selection/list endpoints query active memberships and eligible tenants, not all tenants. Platform tenant list/detail/update requires its existing platform permission and intentionally operates without selected-tenant header; authorized platform reads are not forced through ordinary tenant membership. Public UUID mapping does not broaden platform privileges or add tenant DELETE/create capabilities beyond existing routes.
5. Immutable `public_id`/`id` is not editable through create/update mass assignment. Create accepts authorized parent UUIDs and normal domain properties, with no client-selected row UUID. Derive tenant ownership from context; a public tenant selector is never a writable ownership column. Explicit endpoint-specific tenant creation remains platform-authorized. Existing null/omitted semantics for optional parents must be preserved; no fake zero/nil UUID for absent parents.

Current controls/evidence: [tenant access helpers:14](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/tenant_access.py:14), [product helper:20](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/tenant_access.py:20), [SBOM helper:26](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/tenant_access.py:26), [component helper:32](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/tenant_access.py:32), [logical helper:18](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/logical_sbom_service.py:18), [tenant matching:86](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/auth_context_service.py:86), [membership-bound selection:163](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/auth_context_service.py:163), [integer context guard:143](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/db.py:143), [identity-map enforcement:153](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/db.py:153), [platform bypass of selector:470](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/core/security.py:470), [platform tenant detail permission:798](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/routers/platform.py:798), [dashboard hierarchy checks:67](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/services/dashboard_scope.py:67). These are existing source controls; a future public resolver is not yet implemented.

## Indexed resolution and integer joins

Propose one unique B-tree index on each new per-table public_id. For owned data, include the authorized integer tenant predicate even with globally unique UUIDs. A separate tenant/public_id composite index is not automatically required; justify it with representative plans. Keep current integer PK/FK indexes, composite tenant constraints and integer joins. No DDL/index creation or benchmark is performed here.

Detail reads can substitute a UUID predicate into the existing SELECT and select the needed row directly, avoiding a mandatory resolve-ID query followed by an identical row fetch. A product detail projection can join project/tenant and outer-join current/latest source aliases on integer keys to project public UUIDs. A component SELECT can join its source and tenant and left-join duplicate component on integer keys. Validate tenant/parent predicates on joined aliases, not just the root row. UUID columns do not change authorization or deletion visibility.

For multi-parent inputs, resolve and validate authorized parent combinations with one joined query where practical, retaining resolved rows/IDs for the transaction. Cursor anchors can be a scoped scalar subquery/CTE in the existing list statement, projecting anchor availability where necessary to distinguish invalid anchor from an empty page. Lists should select/project related public IDs through existing joins/aliased joins or eager-loaded projections; avoid serializer-triggered lazy loads. SQL aliases are needed for parent/source/converted/current/duplicate self-links. Internal ordering continues to use integers/timestamps/version rules, not random UUID sorting.

There is **no requirement for an extra round trip per UUID or per list row**. If an endpoint's current shape cannot express the required projections, a bounded batch mapping query per resource type/page is a possible explicit tradeoff, not a claim of zero extra statements. Resolve errors/metadata in bulk as well; never issue a separate lookup for each JSON leaf. Measure query counts/plans and lock behavior before choosing. Existing product `_summary` already issues repeated latest/count/current SQL ([products.py:47](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/routers/products.py:47)); retaining integer joins alone does not fix that. Logical list batches version rows ([logical_sboms.py:86](/Users/ferozebasha/sbom/app/routers/logical_sboms.py:86)); preserve batching while adding public projections. No latency/throughput improvement or fixed query-count ceiling is asserted without measurements.

Readiness for optimized implementation requires the W1 column/index generation design, authorized isolated target and plans/query traces on synthetic data. No additional broad index or ORM relationship loading strategy is mandated by this document alone.

## Tenant selection, clients and compatibility

**Tenant selector proposal:** keep header name `X-Tenant-ID`; its public value becomes a tenant UUIDv4 string. Resolve it once through the existing membership/state flow to integer context. `tenant_id` in related DTOs and available/active tenant objects is the same stable public mapping. Keep missing-header auto-single/selection-required behavior and platform-route header removal; an invalid/stale selector must not silently choose a different tenant. Current public aliases that accept slug/external tenant IDs need explicit retirement or separate namespaced contract review. They are not assumed preserved by a UUID-only local selector.

The browser session key/cookie `sbom_active_tenant_id` is currently digit-only. Proposed future storage uses canonical tenant UUID strings with a versioned transition. Cookie syntax is only input validation; backend membership remains authoritative. A stale numeric value must be intentionally cleared with tenant reselection or converted via an approved pre-cutover transition. A new public numeric translation endpoint would violate the target and is not proposed. Preserve platform header stripping and EventSource's same-origin BFF derivation; no tenant secret or bearer token in query strings.

**Identity/JWT dependency:** native/external principal resolution, JWT sub, token claims, user/member IDs and issuance/refresh semantics are explicitly deferred to a separate identity contract task. W0.2 does not require changing sub to user UUID, interpreting a tenant UUID as sub, reissuing tokens or altering authentication. During future implementation tenant UUID resolution must coexist with the established principal and integer context until the later approved identity plan. An auth-context response may have core tenant UUID fields alongside out-of-scope user/member fields during development; that is not authorization for a partially leaking public production cutover.

Existing numeric bookmarks/URLs, router params, query strings, dropdowns, query keys, Maps/Sets and persisted selections are breaking compatibility surfaces. Keep route shapes/alias spellings for design purposes while making their scoped ID values UUID strings. Do not accept decimals as UUIDs or silently fall back to integer lookup after public cutover. Choice of versioned API rollout, old-link expiry, redirect/transition window and alias retirement is unresolved; any pre-cutover compatibility mechanism must be separately reviewed and cannot remain as an integer-exposing public endpoint at final cutover. Never derive public UUIDs in JavaScript from internal IDs.

## Values deliberately preserved and payload review gaps

Preserve numeric page/page_size/limit/offset, X-Total-Count, component/SBOM/version counts, lifecycle_revision, expected_version/row_version/schema_version/role_assignment_version counters, timestamps, status/enum values (including project_status), scores/priorities and numeric version components. Preserve SBOM/product/component version labels and canonical domain keys. A field named version_id selecting a database row is different and maps to source UUID. CVE/advisory IDs, bom-ref, SPDX identifiers, PURL/CPE, hashes for integrity, semantic provider/role codes and external IAM issuer/subject/tenant metadata remain domain/external values. The separate sbom_type FK remains an explicit unresolved dependency, not an excluded numeric quantity.

Raw SBOM document bytes and vendor package/component references are evidence: do not rewrite bom-ref, PURL or document-level serial/UUID identifiers to database public_id. App-generated envelopes, links and projected local row references must be translated. If a historically generated document already includes local integer references, determine provenance and retention/regeneration policy in a later export/history task rather than editing original evidence in place. Public UUIDs are not SBOM document serial numbers.

Open `metadata_json`, processing_eligibility, dedupe/normalization/lifecycle/compare reports, audit values, validation messages, component evidence and exported JSON may contain local references in values or keys. The explicit field appendix cannot certify all arbitrary JSON/text. Future serializers must map known typed core references or omit internal metadata; unresolved/deleted referenced rows require a documented history/tombstone policy. Never serialize raw ORM dictionaries, globally translate every number, invent a missing UUID mapping, or fall back to integer output. Scope-relevant dynamic containers can be located in audit 05's open-container section; absence from the fixed-reference filter below is an evidence limit.

## Small future acceptance-test matrix

All cases are proposed, not executed; use approved isolated synthetic fixtures only.

| Case | Required assertion |
| --- | --- |
| A01 — Six identities / writes | All six public UUIDs are stable v4/variant-correct, immutable and unique per table across repeated GET/list/update and writer paths; PK/FK integers unchanged. Client-supplied own ID rejected; new source version/restore uses its own mapping. |
| A02 — Nested/list/detail | Product project/tenant/current/latest IDs, logical latest_version IDs, source lineage/conversion parents and component duplicate/source IDs resolve to the correct UUID namespace; nulls/order/response shape preserved; no integer shadows. |
| A03 — Parent consistency | Foreign-tenant and same-tenant wrong-parent selectors, product/current source mismatch, component/source mismatch and forged tenant ownership fail closed; internal services receive only validated integer IDs. |
| A04 — Non-disclosing errors | Malformed/numeric/nil/wrong-version UUID → proposed 422; valid unknown/foreign/wrong-kind resource → same generic 404; unknown/unauthorized tenant selector → same 403; no SQL/internal IDs/body/token echoes. Verify auth/permission precedence separately. |
| A05 — Tenant context | UUID X-Tenant-ID/cookie/session selects eligible membership; disabled/revoked membership fails; absent-header selection rules and platform header removal preserved; no JWT sub/claims change in this task. |
| A06 — Aliases / CRUD | projectid/project_id accept same UUID, reject conflict under proposed rule; aliases/hidden routes consistent; create/update/delete codes and existing lifecycle/soft-delete/permanent safeguards unchanged; no new CRUD capabilities. |
| A07 — Pagination / domain values | UUID cursor anchors preserve integer descending order/no duplicates between unchanged pages; unavailable cursor handled consistently; counts/version/status/CVE/bom-ref/PURL/raw bytes unchanged. |
| A08 — Frontend compatibility | Routes, forms, dropdowns, filters, query keys and BFF streams handle canonical strings without Number/parseInt; numeric persisted state follows agreed explicit reset/transition; no silent tenant change. |
| A09 — Secondary/core references | The scoped field appendix covers bodies/query/form/path/nested aliases/errors/headers/SSE/export projections; dynamic fixture IDs/keys are safely mapped; other-resource contracts remain release dependencies. |
| A10 — Query/authorization regression | Representative joined projections do not add per-UUID/per-row lookup queries; measure bounded batch tradeoffs where used. Preserve TenantSession identity-map, integer join/lock/constraint semantics and direct-API negative authorization behavior. No speed claim without data. |

## Unresolved decisions and later dependencies

| Ref | Decision / dependency | Readiness consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D01 — W1 target/storage | Disposable D does not yet exist/has no creation approval; production unknown; B drift including missing VEX FK still requires disposition. UUID generation/default/backfill/index/immutability mechanics not yet designed or executed. | Storage/execution NO-GO. Carry W0.1 gates; six ADD_UUID4 proposals do not authorize migration. |
| D02 — contract ratification | Proposed 422 syntax/read-only/conflicting-alias behavior, generic 404 for unavailable supplied list filters, 403 tenant selector, and policy evaluation ordering require API review. | Do not assume current clients/errors already behave this way. Confirm canonical grammar and validation sanitizer. |
| D03 — tenant compatibility | Current selector accepts decimal, slug, external mapping; decide explicit transition/retirement and header/cookie/storage version strategy. Missing-header selection must stay intentional. | Must precede tenant-selector implementation/cutover; do not silently keep numeric fallback. |
| D04 — identity/JWT | Principal/user/member identifiers, native/external JWT sub/claims and token issuance/refresh/expiry plan are a separate later contract. | No token redesign now. Coordinate auth-context tenant projections without claiming full identity API leak-freedom. |
| D05 — remaining resources | sbom_type lookup, runs/findings, VEX/assignees, reports/exports/cache/PDF, jobs/sessions and recommendation IDs need their own approved public contracts. Only their references to the six core entities are covered here. | Cross-resource endpoints cannot declare final no-leak readiness from W0.2 alone. PDF run namespace and other out-of-scope ambiguities are not resolved here. |
| D06 — aliases/consumers | API versioning, old numeric URL/bookmark behavior, alias retirement, external clients and numeric persisted browser state reset/transition require decisions. | No dual integer/UUID public contract at final cutover. No new migration/redirect endpoint is assumed approved. |
| D07 — scope naming | Legacy application_ids means projects on one family and product on hierarchy pickers; scope-discriminated IDs need exact endpoint-specific mapping. | Keep documented semantics or approve a separate rename; do not globally substitute products for application. |
| D08 — cursor/history | Last-source-UUID cursor proposal needs filter/snapshot/deleted-anchor semantics; deleted/historical JSON refs, tombstones, immutable files and raw-document retention unresolved. | Future pagination/export/history contract and fixtures required; no integer fallback or destructive rewrite. |
| D09 — performance / coverage | New columns/indexes and join/eager-loading/batch strategy need actual plans/query counts on approved synthetic data. Static inventory is not complete dynamic JSON/TypeScript dataflow/runtime evidence. | No unmeasured performance promises. Review cross-tenant cache keys, nested projections and dynamic containers in later tests. |

Scope ends here: no decisions above are treated as resolved by this report. Next work can ratify this six-entity contract and design W1 on the proposed isolated B-shaped baseline; implementation/testing/cutover still require the dependent approvals and evidence. This task adds documentation only.

## Verification

Only `docs/uuid-migration/phase2/01-core-public-uuid-contract.md` was created. Scoped binding counts, registration/alias coverage, exact source-link bounds and preservation hashes were checked. All acceptance cases above remain future work. No existing file, code, token, migration or database was changed; no service, Alembic command, clone, application test or load test was run; no branch switch/commit/push occurred.

Verification results: six entity rows; 964 field-binding rows; 263 registration-index rows; 19 frontend consumer groups; 1,676 distinct source/audit link locations within existing files. All 32 pre-existing modified/untracked files and the tracked binary diff matched the task-start preservation snapshot; whitespace checks passed. These are documentation coverage/preservation checks, not application acceptance results.
